# Audit — closed defects

The closed rows (fixed, superseded, won't fix) of the defect list begun by the audit of 2026-09-22. The open and
watch rows, the severity and status key and the audit's summary are in `docs/audit-2026-09-22.md`; D-numbers are
shared by both files and never reused.

| D42 | L | fixed `88c0e6df` | The Animator preview's speed test (`sockets.e2e.ts`, "Animator preview speed") measures ×0.5 as 0.52–0.54 (limit ±0.02) on this host, also on the 24.6 commit `ada7321` (checked 2026-09-28 in a worktree, 2/2 at 0.531): the preview clip time runs slightly faster than half its stepped time (found running the 24.7 gate). Not a product bug, fixed in the test 2026-09-29: the preview writes its clip time and elapsed time to the page every 100 ms, so the test's first sample after moving the slider could predate the change and up to 0.1 s at ×1 fell into the ×0.5 window (0.5 + 0.05/0.85 at most, 0.53 on average, as measured); the machine and the preview advance by the same `dt`. The test now starts each window from a sample taken after the change (tolerance unchanged): ×0.5 measures 0.4999–0.5001, ×1 1.0000 (7 of 8 runs; the 8th failed elsewhere, not diagnosed) | `tests/e2e/sockets.e2e.ts` `rateOf` |
| D43 | L | fixed `b489d7ad` | Under load (the fast gate's vitest, 2026-09-28) the two-process claim race "absent claim: fresh project" failed once: the losing process crashed with `EEXIST: mkdir …/projects/demo-0001/.thirdlight` instead of being refused; 4/4 passes alone. `createDirectories` tests `errno === 'EEXIST'`, but Node puts the string in `code` (errno is numeric), so a concurrent creator is reported as an I/O error; the non-recursive `.thirdlight` mkdir that threw was not located (found running the 24.8 gate). Fixed in 24.9: `ensureThirdlightDir` (session.ts, the `.thirdlight` mkdir) and the recovery `ensureDir` compared `errno` with the string; both use `errnoOf` (the `code`) now | `workspace/src/service.ts` `createDirectories`; `tests/ownership-claim-processes.test.ts` |
| D44 | L | fixed `b489d7ad` | `tools/gate.sh`'s vitest step reruns failed files alone by grepping `^ FAIL  <file>`, but vitest colours the line (`ESC[41m ESC[1m FAIL …`), so the rerun never happens and a one-off load flake (D43) makes the fast gate RED (found running the 24.8 gate). Fixed in 24.9 (the escapes are stripped before matching) | `tools/gate.sh` `build_and_unit` |
| D45 | L | fixed `3fa7f99b` | The manifest document (`manifest.json` of Play and the export) put `dialogue` before `modes`, `timelines`, `eventCues` and `shell`, not in `MANIFEST_KEYS_V2` order: `captureManifestV2` wrote its object literal's order, while the buildId preimage used the list, so every reader still verified and nothing failed. Fixed in 25.1 (the document is written in the list's order; found by the every-optional-key test, 2026-09-28) | `project-model/src/manifest-v2.ts` `captureManifestV2` |
| D46 | M | fixed `4dff1765` | In 2D a falling player pressed against a fixed wall on its **left** was held there: Rapier 0.20.0's sweep hit the wall and kept almost none of the fall (0.1 mm per step), so the player crept down any left wall for seconds; the same wall on the right let it fall. Fixed in 25.4: a falling sweep that keeps less than half its fall with only fixed vertical walls pressed into is swept again without the part into the wall, with the capsule narrowed by half the skin so it cannot graze the wall; a mover's side keeps Rapier's response (found by the 25.4 e2e on a stacked wall, 2026-09-28) | `physics-rapier/src/port.ts` `wallSlideRetry` |
| D47 | M | fixed `4dff1765` | A mover with a polygon collider moving sideways into the 2D player fail-stopped the game ("collision correction out of the contracted bound"): the runtime's mover push only knew box colliders, so nothing moved the player out of the kinematic polygon. Fixed in 25.4 (the push uses the polygon's exact extent across the player's box; found checking plan §2's polygon push, 2026-09-28) | `runtime/src/blocks.ts` `pushPolygon` |
| D48 | L | fixed `78b961aa` | Ended play records stayed in the backend's memory for its whole lifetime, each holding its frozen snapshot (the whole scene) and, once the snapshot route had served it, its JSON bytes; a long-running backend grew by one scene per Play (found in 25.5). Fixed in 25.24: a stopped play releases its snapshot, its bytes and any `play.started` still held for a detached owner, and only the last 64 ended plays, none older than an hour, keep their `ended` answer (`play-retention.test.ts`, 2026-09-28) | `backend/src/play.ts` `PlayManager.finishStop`, `pruneEnded` |
| D49 | M | fixed `34847aec` | A spot light shone from 1 m above its object and, unless it pointed straight down, in the wrong direction (from that point towards `direction`), in Play, the export and the Scene view: three starts a new `SpotLight` at (0, 1, 0) (`Object3D.DEFAULT_UP`) and the light was added under its object without resetting it. A hemisphere light under its object took its sky direction from the object's world position (tilted anywhere but above the origin); the Scene view and the bake already took +Y. Fixed in 25.8 (spot at its object's origin; hemisphere hangs off the scene, as ambient; found by the 25.8 cookie e2e, 2026-09-28) | `three-adapter/src/adapter.ts` `localLightOf`, `realizeEntity`; `editor/src/viewport/viewport.ts` `makeSceneLight` |
| D50 | M | fixed `97c45159` | In 3D a character riding a rising mover sank into it: the grounded 3D controller's small fall (gravity × one step) was swept together with the platform's carry, but the port poses movers after the character's sweep, so each step ended that fall inside the risen platform (measured: 0.16 m lost per metre risen at 1 m/s, the character's feet inside the lift). Fixed in 25.12: while what it stands on lifts it, its own fall is cancelled (`mover-signals.test.ts` in `m25-movement`: the gap to the lift stays within 6 cm over a 2 m ride, 2D and 3D; found by the 25.12 lift test, 2026-09-29) | `runtime/src/runtime.ts` `stageCharacterMove` |
| D51 | M | fixed `ba020ddf` | The run digest of `tl_game_observe` (25.16) and the worker-parity step digest hashed the **interpolated** transforms (`forEachInterpolated`, blended with the step before by the last frame's alpha), so in a real Play a run and its replay gave different digests whenever something moved at the observed step — the alpha depends on when frames came; the simulation itself was identical (same positions to the bit). Fixed in 25.17: both hash the committed transforms (`Runtime.forEachCommitted`; found by the 25.17 runner's first run: equal positions, different digests, 2026-09-29) | `game-host/src/step-digest.ts`, `runtime/src/runtime.ts` `forEachCommitted` |
| D52 | L | fixed `9a314cf6` | A single-cell block turned a quarter turn in a layer whose cells are not square in plan (cell size x ≠ z) is drawn and collides at the unturned cell's size turned (its x and z extents swap), so it does not fill its cell and its sides no longer match the neighbours' for hidden faces. Found while building sloped cells in 25.20 (2026-09-29); sloped cells stretch their turned look back to the cell (fixed for them), flat cells left open: the fix changes the meshes and colliders of existing layers with such cells (their replays) — owner's call. **Owner 2026-10-03:** closed by a rule instead, cells must be square from above (x = z). Fixed in 28b.1 by the rule: the block-layer validator refuses x ≠ z, the Inspector edits x and z as one value, and the sloped cells' stretch-back is gone | `project-model/src/block-mesh.ts` `meshBlockChunk` (`classify` with the unturned footprint) |
| D53 | M | fixed `4b0aa347` | A script importing a script library could not be published from its Script tab when the library's digest had never been acknowledged — a library saved while no script imported it is never asked for, and the publish acknowledged only the script's own digest, so the command's refusal (naming the library digest) ended as a bare `behavior_trust_unacknowledged` error with no way to acknowledge it in the editor. Fixed in the phase 25 acceptance test: a refusal naming a digest is asked for (the same trust notice) or, once acknowledged, recorded and the publish retried; the source route's refusal now carries the digest to the editor (`script-publish.test.ts`, `starter-capabilities.e2e.ts`; found by that test, 2026-09-29) | `editor/src/session/script-publish.ts` (moved out of `App.tsx` `publishScript`), `session/client.ts` `publishBehaviorSource` |
| D54 | L | fixed `4b0aa347` | The Behaviors tab's declaration editor would not remove a behavior's last property (its remove button was disabled at one), so a script with no properties — e.g. one made only of callbacks — could not be declared in the editor, though the model allows an empty declaration. Fixed in the phase 25 acceptance test: the last property can be removed; the declaration is created with none (`starter-capabilities.e2e.ts`; found by that test, 2026-09-29) | `editor/src/ui/DeclarationEditor.tsx` |
| D55 | M | fixed `08e60471` | A small crafted PNG sent to KTX2 encode or texture-array pack could inflate to gigabytes: the backend's PNG decoder ran `inflateSync` on the IDAT stream with no `maxOutputLength` (the width × height check bounds the pixels, not the stream), and the encoder's worker thread had no `resourceLimits`. Fixed 2026-09-29: the inflate is capped at the exact size the header implies (every pass's rows with their filter bytes, Adam7 included; one byte more is refused), a pack decodes only the sources a channel reads and bounds them by the first one's size, the worker runs with heap and stack limits (`texture-encode.test.ts`: a 1.6 MB stream that inflates to ~258 MB is refused by encode, pack and the pure inflate with under 16 MB allocated; found by the post-phase review) | `project-model/src/png-decode.ts` `decodePngRgba`; `backend/src/texture-encode.ts` |
| D56 | M | fixed `08e60471` | Two PNG decoders: the backend's (25.19, Node zlib, Adam7, tRNS key) copied the one in project-model (heightmap import, pure inflate, no interlacing). Fixed 2026-09-29: one decoder in project-model (every colour type and depth, tRNS, Adam7, the D55 cap, a caller-chosen pixel bound and an optional inflate), the backend imports it through the `@thirdlight/project-model/png` subpath (a value import the boundary check allows for that subpath only); the copy is deleted (found by the post-phase review) | `project-model/src/png-decode.ts`, `tools/check-boundaries.mjs` `valueSubpaths` |
| D57 | M | fixed `b7979b20` | Limits defined more than once (post-phase review, 2026-09-29): `MAX_TRANSITION_FADE` (runtime and project-model), 256 texture layers in three places (`KTX2_LAYERS_MAX`, `TEXTURE_PACK_LAYERS_MAX`, `MAX_TEXTURE_LAYERS`), `MAX_MATERIAL_INSTANCE_DEPTH` (three-adapter), `AUDIO_MAX_REGISTERED_ASSETS` (game-host), a literal 32 for `SCRIPT_LIBRARY_LIMITS.libraries` (`workspace/src/behavior.ts`), per-kind literals in `commands/src/content-ops.ts`. Fixed 2026-09-29 (26.1): each is defined once in project-model and imported (the `@thirdlight/project-model/limits` subpath for types-only edges, runtime re-exports for the host and renderer), with the other copies found by a sweep (about 15 limits, the id regex, MCP schema bounds); the per-project caps among them (audio 64, libraries 32) go in 26.5 | `project-model/src/limits.ts`, `content-limits.ts`, plan-phase-26 §7 |
| D58 | M | fixed `1b128562` | Process: "split before growing" was not followed after the rule landed: `project-model/src/content.ts` 2629 → 2780 lines, `descriptors.ts` 2137 → 2213, `editor/src/session/client.ts` 2390 → 2417, `runtime/src/types.ts` 2469 → 2489, each without a split (post-phase review, 2026-09-29). Fixed 2026-09-29 (26.1): each split by area into modules re-exported from the original (content 777, descriptors 153, types 323, client 773 + client-core 1,667 lines) | those files; plan-phase-26 §7 |
| D59 | M | fixed `a15a994e` | History comments: phase 25 added ~1,390 source lines in `packages/` and `tools/` naming phases, item ids, dates or `§` (550 after the rule, e.g. `backend/src/play-content.ts`, `mcp-adapter/src/tools.ts` `// ---- phase 25.17`), and test names carry them (post-phase review, 2026-09-29). Fixed 2026-09-29 (26.1): every comment and test title in `packages/*/src`, `tools/` and `tests/` cleaned (emitted JavaScript byte-identical); `tools/history-comments.mjs` in `npm run build` refuses new ones | `packages/*/src`, `tools/` |
| D60 | L | fixed `dc442d71` | History in string literals: MCP tool descriptions (`mcp-adapter/src/tools.ts`, "(phase 25.17)" and others), error hints citing spec sections (`workspace/src/errors.ts`, `commands/src/errors.ts`, `game-host/src/host.ts`, the check tools), the generated export bundle header (`exporter/src/export-bundle.ts`) and `REMOVED_IN_PHASE_24`'s message still name phases, packets or `§` (found by 26.1, which changed comments only). Fixed 2026-09-30 (26.14): every such string (116 found) rewritten to say what it means (the MCP descriptions, error messages and hints, protocol field notes, the bundle header, `REMOVED_FROM_ENGINE`; three messages the M2 contract fixtures pin changed there too), and `tools/history-comments.mjs` now also reads the string literals, template parts and JSX text of package sources (tests excluded), so they fail the build like comments | `packages/*/src`, `tools/` |
| D61 | M | fixed `51fc3260` | Content validation is quadratic in the asset count: `validateContentV3Value` builds the asset-kind map by calling `ktx2TextureIds(doc)` (a scan of every asset) once per asset (`project-model/src/content.ts:313`; `:652` also calls it per glyph). Under today's caps it costs little; on the 26.2 scale bench with the caps lifted (18,000 assets) it is 3.9 s of a 4.7 s validation, and every command re-validates the whole project, so a scene edit takes 11 s and a content edit 26 s (found 2026-09-29). Fixed 2026-09-30 (26.4 A): the derived id, kind and KTX2 sets are made once per asset list, and a command's validation trusts the lists and records it left as they were (the block before is valid), runs the cross-reference rules only when a section they read changed, and composes a scene again only when it or the content it reads changed; full size (caps lifted): scene edit p50 8 ms, content edit 23 ms, open 0.8 s | `project-model/src/content.ts`; 26.4/26.8 |
| D62 | L | fixed `8fab4d51` | Flaky e2e: `inspector.e2e.ts` "a content table … sends a second edit on top of a first still on its way (event sounds)" fails about 1 run in 3 on the GPU host, also at `71c42403` without later changes (2 of 6 runs): the second `setEventCues` (the asset choice made while the first is held) never lands and the poll times out. Either the editor's own-command queue drops or conflicts the second edit under that timing, or the test's held WebSocket/route ordering races; not yet told apart (found 2026-09-29 in 26.3 B's fast gate). Fixed 2026-09-30, a test race: the second edit is built on top of the first, so it also named `neutral-test` and hit the test's hold route just as the test unrouted; Playwright left that request unanswered and the one-at-a-time command queue waited on it (trace: expectedRevision 4, merged args, status -1). The test now holds only the first matching command and keeps its route; 60/60 under 10 workers (was 1 in 40) | `editor/src/session/own-commands.ts`, `tests/e2e/inspector.e2e.ts` |
| D63 | M | fixed `3eeb70bc` | Since the asset records live in the sidecars (26.4 A), a `.tlasset` deleted (or lost to a bad merge) while the project is closed takes its asset out of the project: the next open reads no record for it, and a scene or resource that uses it no longer resolves, so the open is refused ("scene model reference resolves to no catalog record"), naming the use, not the missing sidecar. While the project is open the file check writes it back from the session. Wanted: the open finds an asset file whose sidecar is gone but whose id a scene or resource uses, and says so (or restores the record from a rebuildable cache of the index) instead of refusing on the reference (found 2026-09-30 in 26.4 A's tests). Fixed 2026-09-30 (26.4 B): the open puts a lost sidecar back from the record cache (`cache/records/<id>.tlasset`, a copy of every sidecar written), else re-imports a file named for a missing id that something uses (default settings); both reported in Problems. The rest is D64 | `workspace/src/asset-recovery.ts`, `store-v4.ts` |

Found 2026-10-01 when the scale bench gained KTX2 models (`editor-scale.e2e.ts` failed: 200 model files read again on the second open). Fixed 2026-10-01: the probe asks an `OffscreenCanvas` where there is no document, so the worker transcodes to the GPU's compressed format like the page; `editor-scale` (bench, now with KTX2 and WebP inside models) fails without the fix. Not fixed: why an RGBA-transcoded KTX2 stops the worker's renderer (a GPU without compressed formats would still meet it) Fixed 2026-10-02 (28.1): three's KTX2 loader wraps an RGBA transcode as a `CompressedTexture` of `RGBAFormat`; WebGPU has no block size for it and throws inside the draw (the worker's renderer drew nothing after), WebGL sends it as a compressed upload. The shared loader now hands RGBA transcodes over as `DataTexture`s with their levels, and mip streaming keeps them as data (`StreamedDataTexture`); e2e `ktx2-rgba-thumbnails` and the RGBA case of `texture-streaming` force the path on both renderers | `three-adapter/src/ktx2.ts` |
| D105 | H | fixed `132f7dcd` | Images inside model files could never stream: a GLB's images could only stay inside the file, decoded whole with the model (27.6 counted them; nothing could make them texture assets), and every model carrying the same image held its own copy (Skyforge E48: 945 MB of model bytes). Fixed 2026-10-01: the model import setting "extract textures" (on for new models) makes them KTX2 texture assets the model draws with, streamed by mip and shared; e2e `extract-textures` (a model drew the white stand-in in Play until the capture shipped the textures with their model) | `backend/src/model-textures.ts`, `three-adapter/src/gltf-loader.ts`, `project-model/src/capture.ts` |
| D107 | M | fixed `5063feb7` | A restart (the replay control, the pause panel's restart, `ctx.lifecycle.restart`) kept the environment blend of the run before: a game that had switched to a preset replayed from that look, so a replay's frames (and its digest) differed from the first run's. Found 2026-10-01 while adding the active scene to the environment director (27.10): `EnvironmentDirector.reset` was never called. Fixed 2026-10-01: a restart resets the look (the first start scene's, no preset); runtime test `active-scene` fails without the fix | `runtime/src/runtime.ts` |
| D108 | M | fixed `46107cc5` | An editor command sent before the editor's client has taken in the change feed's latest revisions (MCP edits made just before) is refused with `revision_conflict` instead of being rebased or retried, so a person editing while a tool edits sees a refusal. Seen 2026-10-01 in 27.11's `editor-window.e2e.ts`, which waits for the client to catch up first (a test sync, not a fix). Not investigated: whether the editor retries or only reports it. Fixed 2026-10-01 (27.17): a command refused with `revision_conflict` because someone else edited first waits for the change feed to reach the backend's revision (bounded, `CHANGE_FEED_CATCH_UP_MS`; past it the state is read again) and is sent once more against it; a whole-document edit whose args were built from the older view is still refused (it would undo the edit in between). The test sync is gone; `editor-window.e2e.ts` holds the MCP edits' change-feed events back (late, and stalled) and an immediate GameObject → Box goes through (fails without the fix) | editor client (`client-core.ts` `command`, `own-commands.ts`) |
| D109 | L | fixed `46107cc5` | An object made from the menu bar while the editor window shows appears in the Hierarchy a moment before it is selected; an Esc in that gap closes the window (restoring the selection it had when it opened) and the late selection of the new object then replaces the restored one. Seen 2026-10-01 in 27.12's `editor-window.e2e.ts` (3 of 3 runs once the preview pane was in), which now waits for the new object's selection before Esc (a test sync, not a fix). Open: whether a selection that lands after the window closed should still apply. Fixed 2026-10-01 (27.17): a selection a command makes when it answers belongs to the view it was asked from; one asked while the window showed and answered after it closed is dropped (`useSelectionAcrossWindow` returns the wrapper, `useSceneEditing` `selectLater`). The wait before Esc is gone; the spec holds the create command's answer back and presses Esc at once (fails without the fix) | editor (`EditorWindow.tsx`, `useSceneEditing.ts`) |
| D110 | L | fixed `e1c2b9ec` | Test infra: e2e and bench backends failed to start now and then with `listen EADDRINUSE` (27.9's `inspector`, 27.13's `editor-scale`). The helpers (`tests/e2e/backend.ts`, `tools/perf/backend.ts`, `start.e2e.ts`) probed a free port with `listen(0)`, closed it and gave it to the backend, which bound it a moment later; meanwhile anything else asking the kernel for a port (the 48 specs' static export servers on port 0, other workers' probes) could be handed the same one, and the two probes of one start could even return the same port. The backend cannot bind port 0 and report back (its origins are fixed in its environment before it starts). Reproduced with `node tools/perf/run.mjs ports` (16 parallel starts × 12 rounds with port-0 servers and client connections churning): 13 of 192 starts lost their port. Fixed 2026-10-01 (27.14): `tools/perf/ports.ts` picks both ports (held together) below the kernel's ephemeral range, which `listen(0)` and client sockets never take, and retries a start that still reports EADDRINUSE on fresh ports (`launchOnFreePorts`, `PORT_ATTEMPTS`); the helpers use it. Same stress: 0 of 192 lost without retries, 0 of 320 with 32 parallel starts | tests (`tests/e2e/backend.ts`, `tools/perf/backend.ts`, `tools/perf/ports.ts`) |
| D111 | L | fixed `46107cc5` | Play reports a run `running` (observe `state`) before the preview's renderer has drawn a frame; a screenshot in that gap is refused (`relay_failed`, "the renderer is still initialising; nothing is drawn yet"). Seen 2026-10-01 in 27.15's runs of `starter-capabilities.e2e.ts` under three workers (2 of 2; passes alone), whose first screenshot followed `running` at once; the spec now asks again while the answer is that refusal (a test sync, not a fix). Open: whether `running` should wait for the first drawn frame, or observe should carry the renderer's state. Fixed 2026-10-01 (27.17): `running` stays the simulation's state; a screenshot asked before the renderer drew its first frame waits for it within half the relay timeout (`answerWithinMs` on the screenshot relay, `relayAnswerWithinMs`, the adapter code `render_not_ready`), and answers `render_not_ready` past it. starter-capabilities' retry is gone; `screenshot.e2e.ts` delays the Play page's WebGPU adapter by 2.5 s and asks at once after `running` (fails without the fix) | editor preview (`screenshot-answer.ts`), protocol, three-adapter (`capture.ts`) |

| D113 | M | fixed `39075ed3` | A relayed input exercise (`tl_input_exercise`, and so `tl_playtest`) on a game without scripts or a character never answered: its plain step sampled no input, so the relay's frames never counted and the call ended in `input_relay_timeout` (an empty project or a scenery-only one, e.g. the version-5 fixture). Four real 2048² albedos (art-factory), before → after: refused (400, the 256 px preview drawn) → made in 33.8 s on 1 encoder thread / 18.1 s on 3, 15.2 MiB (three of them in one ETC1S job: 11.1 s, 1.3 MiB); one layer's job raised the process's resident memory by 442 MiB (the ETC1S 3 × 2048² job: ~1,040 MiB), under the pool's 1,280 MiB a worker, memory-capped run. An array that still cannot be made (layers of two sizes) goes to Problems once in plain words (`texture_array_refused`). | `packages/backend/src/texture-slots.ts`, `packages/backend/src/texture-encode.ts` |

| D339 | M | fixed 7e2fc52b | 2026-10-26 (42.6): the sunset look scene with live probes is washed out, as with baked probes (D333): the probes carry the sky three's own environment lookup gives (≈ 25 irradiance straight up under the procedural sky, measured), and the block ground and walls inside the probes' tiles come out far brighter than with the environment's own diffuse light (`acceptance/42.6-cache-view-sunset-webgpu.jpg`; the plain view alike). Cause not found: how materials take the probes' light against the environment's. Follow-up with 42.7 (materials read the cache) and 42.10 (one bake). Update 2026-10-26 (42.7a): unchanged with materials reading the cache (the scene's only light is the sun, drawn per pixel; `acceptance/42.7-sunset-webgpu.jpg`). | Update 2026-10-27 (42.10): the saved sunset ("Bake lighting", drawn on WebGL 2) looks as the live one does, washed out alike (`acceptance/42.10-sunset-{webgl2,webgpu}.jpg`). Update 2026-10-28 (42.15): now WebGPU medium only. The saved sunset on WebGL 2 low is no longer washed out (`acceptance/42.15-saved-sunset-webgl2.jpg`), nor is WebGL 2 with the sun realtime. On WebGPU the haze covers the sky too and stays with `?surfaceRead=0`, but goes with `?quality=low` on WebGPU (`acceptance/42.15-d339-sunset-webgpu-{on,read0,low}.jpg`). So the cache lookup is not the cause; a medium-tier part is (live probes, or a post pass such as TAAU, bloom, AO or reflections). It is worse at morning and noon, when the sun runs through the day (`42.15-live-walk-sunset-webgpu-strip.jpg`). Update 2026-10-28 (owner review of the 42.15 shots): the owner sees the outdoor scenes as bloom blown out, the interiors as fine. A lead from reading the code, not yet tested: bloom is the medium-tier pass that low drops (`quality-levels.ts`: low has `bloom: { enabled: false }`), and it runs on the scene colour before tone mapping and exposure (`environment-nodes.ts`, `bloom(color, strength * 3, radius, threshold)`, then `renderOutput`). So its threshold (default 0.85, `environment.ts`) is compared with unexposed HDR values, and an outdoor sky and sunlit ground, far above 0.85 and brighter since 42's sky bounce, bloom across the whole frame at strength 0.6 × 3. Test first: the sunset at medium with bloom off. If the veil goes, the fix is to compare the threshold after exposure, with a soft knee, and to review the default strength. Exposure alone can't fix it while bloom runs before it. Update 2026-11-01 (44b.0): still open; with `?quality=medium` forced, WebGL 2 is veiled as WebGPU is (`~/.cache/thirdlight-phase44b/acceptance/before/sunset-webgl2-medium.jpg`), so it is the medium tier on both renderers, not WebGPU; the corridor is fine. 44b.1 fixes it. Update 2026-11-01 (44b.1): cause found, in two parts. (1) Bloom: with it off the veil goes on both renderers (`~/.cache/thirdlight-phase44b/acceptance/44b.1/bloomoff-*.jpg`); it ran before exposure and three's high-pass passed the whole colour of every pixel over the threshold, the composite adding ≈ 9 × strength of it. Fixed: the threshold is compared with the exposed colour, soft knee, only the excess glows. (2) The sunset look is ≈ 2 stops overexposed at its exposure 1 (sky light puts ground and walls several times over the threshold), so the corrected bloom still veils it at 1 and is clean at exposure 0.3 (`44b.1/sunset-webgpu-medium-exposure0.3.jpg`). Open until 44b.3's auto-exposure is seen on the sunset. **44b.3: fixed (bb70d593 + 7e2fc52b).** Cause: bloom compared before exposure (fixed in 44b.1) on a sunset look about 3.5 stops overexposed at its fixed exposure 1. A camera with auto-exposure (engine defaults) settles the sunset at exposure 0.09 on both renderers at medium: no veil, the two renderers alike (`~/.cache/thirdlight-phase44b/acceptance/44b.3/auto-sunset-*-medium.jpg`; the same build at fixed exposure 1 still veiled: `fixed-sunset-*`). The look scene keeps its fixed exposure 1 (the over-bright reference adaptation must handle; default chosen, owner to confirm); a fixed look should author about 0.3.

| D406 | L | fixed 4eac768a | 2026-10-29 (43.5c; seen in 43.5a's fast gate, lights.e2e's lamp room once waited 30 s on `0\|4\|6`): an on-demand host (the editor) stopped drawing with the shadow atlas unsettled, so `data-tl-shadow-atlas`'s per-frame `drawn` stayed on the last frame's count. Reproduced (1 of 6 runs, two workers): stuck on `0\|5\|6` with no GPU submit in 1 s. Cause: (1) a fade took the whole time since the last frame (`dt` after an idle spell or a skipped precompile ≥ 0.25 s), so new tiles faded in within one frame (a pop) and no frame followed; (2) a light whose rank asked for another tile size waited for unrelated frames: after a one-step view change the tiles stayed at the old size (WebGL 2's lamp-room shadows measured that way: 158.6/166.3 darkening vs three's 175.5/176.7). Fix: a frame advances a fade by at most `ATLAS_FADE_STEP_MAX` (1/30 s), and `moving()` asks for frames while a light waits for a resize (not once one found no room). The Scene view draws on demand and asked for frames only while probes were stale; after the sky change a probe placement waited its 1 s throttle (and a sky change seen early waits its turn, tiles wait their upload), the frames stopped, and the next menu click drew the new light. Fixed: the view draws on until those are done, the test waits for the light settled before its crops. Not 44b or r187. Also on main (frozen): carry over at the merge. | `tests/e2e/editor-window.e2e.ts`, Scene view icons over terrain |

Not probes or exposure: WebGPU Play's picture freezes. Frames are still drawn (60 a second, the scene pass in each), but hiding the whole scene leaves the picture unchanged, so the canvas shows a stale frame (no tufts, a dark half-drawn mound). Not the precompile hold (2 runs, none running) and not `installPrecompileTargets`. Cause (traced in Play, render calls and binds logged): the final post quad drew into the terrain macro baker's 64² target, not the canvas. three r187's `compileAsync` builds its collected draws later and ends each by binding the target current when it was called; the baker compiles into its target and puts the renderer's back at once, so the builds re-bound the bake target between frames. Fixed in `installPrecompileTargets` (each build ends with the target last bound since it began); a second freeze in the streamed Play came from a bake draw that threw (D426) and left its target bound: it now restores in `finally`. Alone and in the gate: passes (3.3–3.5 min). | `tests/e2e/terrain-cdlod.e2e.ts`, WebGPU Play picture (r187) |

| D464 | M | fixed 89598c15 | 2026-11-03 (46b.8b, LOD draws, seen on staging): a surface layer thinner than a coarse level's cell shows the material under it in that level: the slice's hills, all grass at level 0, show earth stripes along every contour step at levels 1 and 2 (1 m and 2 m cells), a hard shading jump at the level boundary. Cause: the mip downsample takes the material dominant among a parent's eight cells (grass one cell thick over earth loses), and the shader reads the coarse cell a quarter cell into the surface. Proposed fix: the downsample prefers the materials of the cells at the surface (each column's topmost solid cell in the parent, or cells beside air), a derived version step with an owner note; for 46b.11b/46d. Fixed (46b.8c): the downsample takes the material of the cells nearest the surface (derived 8), and the shader reads 0.125 m deep at every level. Cause: the mip downsample keeps a built cell only when it fills half the node cell (`MIP_ROLE_PRIORITY`), so half-a-cell shells break up. For 46b.9 (detail pieces high → low → none: the cell meshed from its mip shape) and 46b.11b to decide: keep thin shells solid at level 1 (a built cell keeps the parent when it closes an exposed side), or draw built cells at level 0 further. Fixed a0dd0127: walk blocks are packed and sent only while the page reads its GPU walk, packed by words (0.41 → 0.05 ms a block): with the volume on, nothing pending 9.84–10.02 s against the head's 11.06–11.26 (packing 1.45 → 0.28 ms a frame); with the store off nothing is packed, but nothing pending stays 9.94–11.10 s against the base's 7.41–8.67. The stitch's yield after a stop changed nothing (tried, reverted 60a3e93a). Left: the job workers derive less a second after the stop (0.5–0.8 s against 0.7–1.6) while the simulation's ticks carry 13.5–15.0 ms a frame outside any span (base 7.6–8.8; in flight 9.5–11.1 against 4.8–5.6). Next: spans over the rest of the tick to name that time. 2026-11-17 (46e.10c, plan-46e §8c 46e.10c): fixed 5503e495. Named by spans (6a1af73a): the time was the frame encoder reading the runtime's full diagnostics every tick, which walk every voxel world's streamer, far field and pool stats (11.8 ms a tick after the stop; 6.1 at the base), though they ride to the page one frame in 60; the busy tick held the job workers' answers and so their next jobs. A frame now reads the diagnostics' head and builds the full set only when it sends them: store off, nothing pending 6.98–7.34 s against the base's 7.37–7.47 and the head before's 10.51–11.39 (same hour, two rounds each), the tick's unspanned time 0.16–0.17 ms, the derive rate back to the base's | `packages/voxel/src/voxel-streamer.ts`, `packages/runtime/src/voxel-world-host.ts` |

## Process findings

- 14 MB of process documentation (130 handoffs, 244 KB STATUS) for ~80k lines
  of code. Archived under `archive/packet-workflow/`.
- "Accepted" gates were model self-reviews. Acceptance now requires an
  automated browser test plus an owner look (see `AGENTS.md`).
- M2–M4 work had never been committed. Committed as `0adc7aa`.
